# AI Engineer YouTube Topical Wiki

This wiki is compiled from local AI Engineer YouTube transcripts. Start with a topic, follow concept links for source-backed detail, and use source notes when you need the original video context.

## Current Emphasis

- [The review bottleneck is comprehension, not reviewer time](concepts/the-review-bottleneck-is-comprehension-not-reviewer-time.md) - Mike Krieger (Anthropic Labs, previously Anthropic's chief product officer) separates the two constraints most review discussions merge: the team is still bottlenecked on review for architecture-touching changes, but "that's — okay, we can carve out time differently. It's like bottlenecked on human ability to even fully conceptualize what we're doing." Time is reallocatable; comprehension is not, so the fix changes the artifact — an explanation of intent and tradeoffs travelling with the diff — rather than the calendar. The same interview supplies the missing half of this wiki's ambition pages: [ask size lags model capability because early products boxed the model in](concepts/ask-size-lags-model-capability-because-early-products-boxed-the-model-in.md), which makes timid usage a product problem with two levers (grant freedom that looks excessive per user, and teach the larger ask), and it names the surface that spreads larger asks — [make delegation multiplayer](concepts/make-delegation-multiplayer-so-people-see-larger-asks.md), because a private CLI hides the org's best prompts. On the build side, an unattended weekend port of a few hundred thousand lines makes the oracle question load-bearing: [validate a cross-language port against production runtime data](concepts/validate-a-cross-language-port-against-production-runtime-data.md), with the incremental cutover boundary as the part the model does not choose. Three organizational entries round it out: [two-week persevere-or-pivot bets with a project-independent org chart](concepts/run-two-week-persevere-or-pivot-bets-with-a-project-independent-org-chart.md), [pre-measure everything and build runtime knobs before you need them](concepts/pre-measure-everything-and-build-runtime-knobs-before-you-need-them.md), and [pace against the cycle, not the day](concepts/pace-against-the-cycle-not-the-day-in-ai-speed-teams.md). On product shape, [surface fragmentation makes the user the integration layer](concepts/product-surface-fragmentation-makes-the-user-the-integration-layer.md) and regulated verticals need you to [draw the cut line between verified data and free-form agent analysis](concepts/draw-the-cut-line-between-verified-data-and-free-form-agent-analysis.md). Nothing in the interview is measured.

- [Satisfaction of search stops agents at the first plausible hit](concepts/satisfaction-of-search-stops-agents-at-the-first-plausible-hit.md) - Peter Werry (Unblocked) borrows a radiology term for the failure this wiki's retrieval pages had no name for: an agent handed a searchable wiki finds one plausible hit and stops, producing a defensible answer from real evidence while missing the documents that would have changed the plan. "Access to information doesn't equal understanding" — attaching a wiki "still doesn't tell the agent where the information is that it needs." It is a stopping failure, not a ranking failure, so it is invisible to recall over what was returned and untouched by a better embedding model. The second half is that [distillation is a separate step from retrieval](concepts/distillation-is-a-separate-step-from-retrieval.md): how dependencies interact is a relation over documents that no document contains. Underneath both is a mental model worth keeping — [an agent is an expert who onboards again on every task](concepts/an-agent-is-an-expert-who-onboards-again-on-every-task.md), so the deficit is organizational rather than intellectual and the cost multiplies by task volume. The cheap mitigation is to [attach sources as a correction surface and a continuation pointer](concepts/attach-sources-as-a-correction-surface-and-a-continuation-pointer.md) — a human fixes the knowledge base, and the agent already knows where to jump next. On the review side, mined PR history is [weighted by the author's expertise](concepts/weight-mined-review-guidance-by-the-authors-expertise.md) using the same graph that maps thin review coverage, and a sharp drop in flagged issues turned out to be [a model swap whose only record was a Slack thread](concepts/a-model-swap-moves-agent-output-metrics-and-the-reason-lives-in-chat.md). The talk's own numbers are one unreplicated pair of runs with the wall clock disclaimed, which is why the durable evaluation lesson is to [measure a context layer on compounding, not on the first task](concepts/measure-a-context-layer-on-compounding-not-the-first-task.md).

- [Measure multi-GPU headroom against a communication-aware roofline](concepts/measure-multi-gpu-headroom-against-a-communication-aware-roofline.md) - Simran Arora (Together AI) supplies the number that relocates the bottleneck under every serving and training technique this wiki records: from the A100 to the B200, BF16 tensor core throughput improved 7.2x while intra-node communication improved 3x and inter-node 2x, so "communication is increasingly consuming the majority of the runtime" at scale — and a PyTorch-plus-NCCL baseline sits below 50% of its communication-aware roofline on most of 87 real problems. The three standard escapes each fail differently (collective libraries are built for bulk contiguous transfers, distributed DSLs cannot track hardware churn, hand-tuned operators need five or six months per precision), and what worked was [adding a small primitive set to the single-GPU kernel you already have](concepts/add-multi-gpu-primitives-to-a-single-gpu-kernel.md) — roughly a dozen lines, in production at Together AI and Cursor. Two decisions live inside it: [which transfer mechanism moves the data](concepts/choose-the-inter-gpu-transfer-mechanism-by-message-size-and-resource-cost.md) and [whether compute and communication overlap within one SM or across SMs](concepts/overlap-communication-with-compute-intra-sm-or-inter-sm.md). The second half turns that hand-derived rubric on frontier models and reports a sharp negative: they compile after retries and then cannot make any of those choices, [succeeding mainly on the patterns public code already contains](concepts/models-solve-the-parallelism-patterns-the-internet-already-contains.md), with [more samples buying correctness and not speed](concepts/more-samples-buy-correctness-not-speedups.md) — and the gap survives putting the principles in the context window.

- [Match the inference lever to the latency metric it moves](concepts/match-the-inference-lever-to-the-latency-metric-it-moves.md) - Red Hat's llm-d team separates two serving optimizations by the number each one defends: KV-cache-aware routing buys time-to-first-token by not doing prefill at all, while prefill/decode disaggregation buys inter-token latency by removing the stall a bursty compute-bound prefill inflicts on memory-bound decode sharing its GPU (P99 ITL ~900 ms aggregated against ~100 ms disaggregated). What keeps this from being advice to disaggregate is the prerequisite — the decode worker pulls the KV cache over the network, so "you must possess an advanced high-speed network fabric like RDMA or RoCE" — and the underlying regime change, that the client now owns the prompt structure and therefore the server's cache lifecycle.

- [Map the agentic commerce protocol stack by layer](concepts/map-the-agentic-commerce-protocol-stack-by-layer.md) - Ahnaf Prio (Best Buy) untangles a pile of acronyms into four layers that compose: MCP advertises a merchant's capabilities, A2A carries the message between a customer agent and a merchant agent, ACP (OpenAI) and UCP (Google) supply the commerce vocabulary, and AP2 carries payment authority — all four visible in one demo turn. Only ACP and UCP genuinely compete, and even they share a call shape, so the cost is a serializer rather than a second integration. Underneath sits the reason domain protocols exist at all, stated in one line that generalizes far past shopping: "there is not much of a difference between adding an item to cart, adding a second quantity. But to us merchants, that's a second line item" — a buyer's interface collapses distinctions the seller's systems treat as structurally different, so [an agent protocol has to carry the counterparty's vocabulary](concepts/agent-protocols-must-encode-the-distinctions-the-ui-collapses.md), not the user's simplified one. The rest is a merchant's cost sheet: [both major specs require you to push a product feed rather than answer catalog searches](concepts/push-a-product-feed-because-catalog-search-does-not-scale.md), in three different schemas, resynced continuously, because the aggregator wants the index — and the reason named first is "sponsored products, retail media related things, ranking"; [checkout is a named state machine](concepts/model-agentic-checkout-as-an-explicit-session-state-machine.md) whose arming transition is payment-method selection; [protocol conformance is a continuous eval whose failure mode is silent de-listing](concepts/eval-agent-surfaces-for-protocol-compliance-not-just-behavior.md) rather than a bug report; and [an unscoped public agent gets used for whatever the model can do](concepts/public-agent-surfaces-get-repurposed-as-free-general-compute.md), leaking discount codes and other customers' activity that no generic guardrail models. Nothing is measured, Best Buy's own implementation never appears, and every support claim is explicitly present-tense.

- [Streaming forecloses the provider fallback](concepts/streaming-forecloses-the-provider-fallback.md) - Kanish Manuja (Twilio) builds a whole LLM-gateway talk out of one string — "something went wrong. Please try again" — and the reason it exists is a commitment you cannot undo: "once you have decided to go with provider A, you have to continue going with provider A. You cannot mid-stream change the providers. Whatever has been sent to the client, it's done." So the streaming decision is not a pure latency win, it is perceived speed bought with the failover you built, and the usable rule is that output a human reads as it arrives should stream while output a program parses whole should not. Around it sits an operations checklist that argues against three reflexes: [per-request fallback rather than retry-and-breaker](concepts/prefer-per-request-fallback-to-retries-and-circuit-breakers-for-llm-calls.md), since retries "eat into your latency budget" and a breaker is wrong when a healthy second provider exists; [more headroom on the fallback provider than the primary](concepts/your-fallback-provider-is-under-tested-and-under-provisioned.md), because it absorbs the whole load at the worst moment; [P99 and timeouts per model class per route](concepts/track-latency-and-timeouts-per-model-class-per-route.md), because "a reasoning model's normal is actually a chat model's outage" and a missing timeout is "the number one root cause of your silent outage"; [guardrails treated as a failable dependency](concepts/treat-guardrails-as-a-failable-dependency-with-a-time-budget.md) with a fail-open/fail-closed decision made in advance; and [decentralizing the gateway while centralizing the governance](concepts/decentralize-the-gateway-centralize-the-governance.md), because most teams asking for one company-wide gateway want cost tracking and rate limits, not shared traffic. Nothing in the talk is measured, so it is a map of unexamined decisions rather than evidence about them.
- [Stage productivity pilots to strip one confound at a time](concepts/stage-productivity-pilots-to-strip-one-confound-at-a-time.md) - Clare Liguori (AWS) reports Amazon internal pilots at "a median of 4.5x productivity improvement and sometimes more than 10x," but the durable part of the talk is how the evidence was built. Three studies ran in sequence, each answering the objection that invalidated the last: a greenfield "pathfinder" team of six top engineers who replaced a 30-person, 18-month estimate in 76 days; a protected 10-day sprint that fixed the staffing objection but ran with no on-call and a backlog a senior engineer had spent three weeks decomposing; and finally 50 ordinary teams in existing brownfield codebases, watched for most of a year, measured on deployment velocity to production rather than commits. In that last study the tool was roughly held constant — "90% of these teams used Kiro" — and the outcome still split, under 3x for half against a median of 4.5x for the rest, which supports "the tool is not sufficient" rather than "the tool does not matter," since no team worked without one. The five habits behind the split are ordinary and interlocking: write down the tacit knowledge the agent lacks *and* prune the workarounds newer models stopped needing; [budget the productivity dip](concepts/budget-the-productivity-dip-that-precedes-the-agent-speedup.md) that brownfield pre-work causes, because "in almost every team that was interviewed, they reported that their productivity actually went down," and because the real failure is a leader asking "why are you not going faster?" mid-investment; stop babysitting, since [being in the loop is the ceiling on agent parallelism](concepts/being-in-the-loop-is-the-ceiling-on-agent-parallelism.md) — a human waiting out 30-second turns cannot occupy a second loop, so feed the task plus "how it can self-validate" and promote those criteria into the steering file; settle intent in a document before arguing with generated code; and shift testing onto fast local deterministic mocks, because loop count is what buys an hours-long unattended run. The closing observation is the one most likely to outlive the numbers: once "the code only takes 1 to two months to write," [approvals become the long pole](concepts/when-code-stops-being-the-long-pole-approvals-become-it.md) — a two-month build decision and a two-month launch review were noise against a 9-12 month build and are now the critical path, so "frontier engineering teams spend more time making decisions than they do writing code." Every figure is self-reported internal Amazon data on output proxies with no quality column, no control arm, and no team reported to have tried and failed.
- [Let the agent reach a test environment, not production, when the domain carries money](concepts/let-the-agent-reach-a-test-environment-not-production.md) - Andrew Garvin demos a coding agent building a complete usage-billing configuration from one sentence — a provisioned customer, metered usage, and a draft invoice split into build, plan mode, cloud, and AI gateway credit pools — and then, as the vendor of the product being driven, argues against letting anyone do that unattended: "the goal that we have from a product development standpoint is not to have a customer operate the entire system without a human in the loop. This is a type of system that is both business critical, has deep business logic behind it." What makes it worth filing is *where* the line falls. The agent has full autonomy inside a sandbox and none outside it, so the constraint is a property of the domain rather than of the model, and it will not loosen the way uncertainty- and reliability-based limits do. Two vendor-side guardrails make the unsupervised stretch usable — portable skills files carrying "the different ways to hit foot guns," and deliberately verbose errors "so that the agent can self-correct" — and the least obvious of the three ideas is a testing one: [seed the sandbox with usage, not just objects](concepts/seed-the-agent-built-sandbox-with-usage-not-just-objects.md), because a provisioned customer and a configured credit rule look identical whether or not they interact correctly, and the draft invoice is the only artifact a human can actually read. The talk's second half is a taxonomy: [separate agent as product, agent as buyer, and agent as user](concepts/separate-agent-as-product-buyer-and-user.md), three projects with three obligations that teams answer one of while believing they answered all three — and the third is the one that arrives at companies that never shipped an agent, because [per-seat pricing loses its referent when one agent does the work of many logins](concepts/per-seat-pricing-loses-its-referent-when-agents-do-the-work.md). HubSpot is cutting its seat price and adding credits in the same move. Nothing in the talk is measured: three growth claims are "exponential" with no figure, no quality data exists for the agent's output, and the live demo failed at initialization.
- [Ship a proof a small kernel can recheck, not a claim you must trust](concepts/ship-a-proof-a-small-kernel-can-recheck.md) - Varun Pant's formal-verification talk earns its place in this wiki on an asymmetry rather than on rigour. An AI converted zlib to Lean in "a week or so" and emitted "32,000 lines of proof" nobody reads, while the thing you have to trust stays a small kernel with open-source implementations "in C++, Rust, Lean" that "you yourself can actually go write" — the only verification property here whose cost does not grow with agent output. His opening audit is worth keeping even by teams who will never write a proof: an LLM judging code "is probabilistic," tests "only check some inputs, not all," and "human code review doesn't scale to match agent speed," so "none of these can say for all inputs the code is correct." What the proof buys is paid for upstream, which is why the second page exists: [validate the specification, because the proof cannot](concepts/validate-the-specification-because-the-proof-cannot.md). Once "humans own the specification and machines own the code and proof," a wrong spec no longer produces a bug — it produces a certified bug, and auto-formalization inserts a second link that the verifier does not check. Notably, testing survives by changing target: the method for validating a spec is "either the human reviews it, or you test that it holds on some inputs." For code that will not be rewritten in a prover, [pick a verification route by which translation you can afford](concepts/pick-a-verification-route-by-which-translation-you-can-afford.md) — rewrite in Lean, keep a model beside the code, annotate in place for Z3 under Verus, translate Rust's MIR with Aeneas, or lower many languages into AWS's work-in-progress Strata core — and see the shipped instance of the second route, where AWS's Cedar [gates releases on agreement between an executable spec and the shipping code](concepts/gate-releases-on-agreement-between-an-executable-spec-and-the-shipping-code.md) with "about 100 million differential random tests run nightly. No version ships until this is satisfied." Nothing in the ten-minute talk is measured, and proof maintenance across changes is never raised.
- [Ship stable APIs and let users vibe-code the interface](concepts/ship-stable-apis-and-let-users-vibe-code-the-interface.md) - DoorDash's GenAI platform team stopped shipping annotation screens. Annotation work varied too much across use cases — grading restaurant menus, image annotation, manual testing — for one UI, and "it's almost hard for a platform team to build like a UI specific for each use case," so they shipped stable APIs and had strategy-and-operations staff point Codex or Claude Code at them: "because we had these APIs we were actually able to enable our stat ops teams to… vibe code their own annotation UIs." The precondition is the part that travels — "what stood out to us was the underlying patterns were similar" — the variation was in the presentation, not the data model. That is the third stage of a progression worth borrowing on its own, [move the platform's primary surface as its users gain tools](concepts/move-the-platforms-primary-surface-as-its-users-gain-tools.md): UI first because the domain experts were not engineers, API first because engineers were "blocked on the central platform," workflow first because coding agents turned non-engineers into API consumers. Judge calibration went the other way and got a first-party self-serve console, where the interesting problem is adoption rather than accuracy — [show the prompt diff so a non-engineer can promote an optimized judge](concepts/show-the-prompt-diff-so-a-non-engineer-can-promote-an-optimized-judge.md), because "a lot of this is a closed box… it's hard to see what's actually happening," answered by rendering the original and calibrated prompts side by side before an owner elevates one. And the owner is not the same role everywhere: [keep judge-prompt ownership configurable while the org is still learning](concepts/keep-judge-prompt-ownership-configurable-while-the-org-is-still-learning.md) records strategy-and-operations, PMs, and engineers each owning the prompt in different teams, treated as an org design still converging. The talk contains no measurements — "a lot of reduction in the spend at per annotation cost" against "thousands of rows that need to get annotated every week" is the entire outcome claim.
- [Tools are the only primitive every client implements](concepts/tools-are-the-only-primitive-every-client-implements.md) - Jesse Lumarie built Figma's first MCP server as a one-day-a-week side project at a company that "didn't really have 20% projects," and the useful part of the retrospective is what he built *against*. Not the spec: "many clients only implemented a subset of the spec, and many features were very experimental," so "the client compatibility matrix from March 2025" was a maintained artifact and three features Figma needed — server instructions, elicitation, sampling — were reimplemented as prompts returned inside tool results. The sharpest line is that presence is not sufficiency: "for sampling even when VS code supported it you could only really query it as a general agent not specific to the codebase." Two representation decisions travel further than MCP. [Pick the serialization the models have seen most](concepts/pick-the-serialization-the-models-have-seen-most.md) is the choice among three ways to hand over the same C++ scene graph — an internal JSX/XML dialect, a React and Tailwind form borrowed from Figma's website builder, and a plain image — settled by pretraining mass rather than fidelity: "we had a hunch that this representation would be the best one because lots of the models were sort of [trained] on this React Tailwind type of code." [Return a pointer to the reader's own component](concepts/return-a-pointer-to-the-readers-own-component-instead-of-a-copy.md) is the rare compression that does not trade against quality, because "an enterprise doesn't care if it's pixel perfect if it's not using its like battle tested accessible and internationalized components" — so mapped elements collapse to "use button component." Two smaller pages carry the operational residue: [optional self-reported tool arguments are segmentation signal](concepts/optional-self-reported-tool-arguments-are-segmentation-signal.md) ("agents lie, but it was at least a signal"), and [an installed desktop app is an auth and filesystem beachhead](concepts/an-installed-desktop-app-is-an-auth-and-filesystem-beachhead.md), which is how Figma shipped before MCP had an OAuth story. Nothing in the talk is measured; the only outcome claim is the unquantified "one of the fastest growing products that they've ever had."
- [Skills are the residual where organizational know-how lands](concepts/skills-are-the-residual-where-organizational-know-how-lands.md) - Imad Touil (QuantumBlack) gets to skills by elimination rather than enthusiasm. A workflow holds four authorable things and three cannot carry knowledge: hooks "trigger on events," MCP servers are consumed from vendors ("who actually build a lot of MCPs? We just use MCP tools… so we don't really own"), and sub agents exist "just to minimize the context window" — so "all of your know-how is actually at the skills level. And if you don't have the right structure of your skills, then you're not really having a deterministic workflow." Three ideas travel with it. [A missing skill is billed as tokens, not recorded as a gap](concepts/a-missing-skill-is-billed-as-tokens-not-recorded-as-a-gap.md) is the most portable: nothing fails when a skill is absent, so the knowledge gets re-derived conversationally by every engineer who needs it — "someone is vibe coding back and forth… burning more tokens from one side cost-wise, but also the productivity is spending more time rather than giving in one shot the right answer" — which makes cost per task class the leading indicator of a library gap, and a team that looks average on output while its spend is an outlier the thing to look for. [Skill composability is decided before authoring, not in the registry](concepts/skill-composability-is-decided-before-authoring-not-in-the-registry.md) separates what a catalog can buy (discovery, ownership, versioning, access control) from what it cannot: two independently written skills over the same slice of a domain are both findable and both owned and still conflict when loaded, so the domain carve and the named human owners come first — "this is where technology stop solving the problem." And [auto-evolving skills multiply whatever governance you already have](concepts/auto-evolving-skills-multiply-whatever-governance-you-already-have.md) is the closing counter to the self-improvement pages here, all of which describe loops that run inside one person's context: scale the loop and the author is no longer the reader, so it accelerates whichever regime exists. Read with the limits attached — nothing in this talk is measured, and its fifteen-team, six-month illustration is a simulation the speaker wrote and parameterized himself.
- [Review comments have two audiences with inverted error costs](concepts/review-comments-have-two-audiences-with-inverted-error-costs.md) - Uber routes GitHub, Phabricator, and its agent loop into one review service, and the sharpest thing that surfaced is that the two readers price the two error types in opposite directions. Inner-loop "accuracy needs actually need to go up, or else we can result in… cavitation of an agent where it fixes something, goes back, gets another code review, and has to kind of like fix backwards because the quality of the comment was low," while "agents are more than happy to go through and fix 100 nits on a pull request where your engineers really get frustrated." A human's skepticism is the layer that absorbs a wrong comment, and the inner loop removes it. Three more ideas travel with the same system, which posts about 25,000 comments a week at a ~67% addressal rate. [Measure a review bot by whether the comment changed the code](concepts/measure-a-review-bot-by-whether-the-comment-changed-the-code.md) starts from "the model doesn't know that it's wrong. It always confidently says 100% sure" and climbs a four-rung ladder from cost and surveys to reply sentiment to addressal to agent trajectory, with coverage as the reason to prefer behaviour over asking: only 10% of comments draw any feedback. [Distributed rule authoring is a platform problem, not an authoring problem](concepts/distributed-rule-authoring-is-a-platform-problem.md) records that teams wrote their review skills in minutes by asking a model to read their past reviews, while "the hard part was how to run these skills at scale with consistent quality and low cost." [Comment volume is a property of the review pipeline, not the model](concepts/comment-volume-is-a-property-of-the-review-pipeline.md) puts the volume decision in one post-processing waist, because no single generator can know it is the fourth one to report the same defect. All figures are self-reported from slides, the 60%-cost/70%-quality headline is a self-comparison against their own earlier build, and the bottleneck metric that motivated the project — time to first review, three hours in 2024 and nine in 2026 — is never reported again after deployment.
- [Async agents need a forking substrate and a user who tolerates out-of-order completion](concepts/async-agents-need-a-forking-substrate-and-a-tolerant-user.md) - Varun Shenoy runs an autonomy ladder from the operator's chair, and the useful part is his account of why one rung is solved only for code. Background coding agents work because of two accidents that belong to software practice rather than to agents: a forking substrate — "we've figured out what the async and forking mechanism for code is. You just spin up a bunch of sandboxes and do work. What does that look like for the rest of the world?" — and a reception habit, since engineers "launch 10 jobs and be comfortable with the fact that job seven might finish before job three" while "people clean out their inbox one email by one email, not 10 emails at once." Three more ideas travel with it, none of them measured. [Operational outcomes are eval labels you only see if you own the operation](concepts/operational-outcomes-are-eval-labels-you-only-see-if-you-own-the-operation.md) describes an eval setting where the world does the scoring — "did the roof get repaired? Did the books get closed?" — which removes the most expensive step in eval construction and leaves a one-bit label that arrives weeks late. [Continual learning and enablement are one loop with a cold start](concepts/continual-learning-and-enablement-are-one-loop-with-a-cold-start.md) makes adoption the rate limiter on the improvement program rather than a concern downstream of it, and names the seed problem: "everyone assumes the usage just shows up… It never does." [Co-design in person because remote channels filter the requirements](concepts/co-design-in-person-because-remote-channels-filter-the-requirements.md) explains why an empty ticket queue is not evidence of a working product.
- [The Best Engineers Adopt Agents Last, and Their Objections Are the Roadmap](concepts/the-best-engineers-adopt-agents-last-and-their-objections-are-the-roadmap.md) - Eyal Blum's report from inside Figma's engineering org inverts the usual reading of agent skepticism. The slowest adopters there are the strongest engineers — the ones "holding together with their mental duct tape all the places that agents are not working well," carrying institutional context that "ha[s] never [been] written down," and therefore meeting every failure first. Their skepticism is calibrated, their complaints are already ordered by what is costing them most, and the move is to put them in charge of the roadmap rather than to argue them onto the tools: "they will come along once they see that the improvement that they're making [is] actually making their life better." Four more ideas travel with it, none of them measured. [Write the test first so the agent cannot fit it to the code](concepts/write-the-test-first-so-the-agent-cannot-fit-it-to-the-code.md) names the cheapest independence axis this wiki has between a generator and its verifier — same model, same prompt, same method, only the order changes — because a test authored after the implementation "will fit the test to the code rather than fit the code to pass the verification criteria." [Structure an agent plan with a frozen why and reviewer-sized phases](concepts/structure-an-agent-plan-with-a-frozen-why-and-reviewer-sized-phases.md) gives the plan document four requirements that hold whatever loop runs it: a goal section the agent may not rewrite, phases sized to a PR you would review without fetching coffee first, a validation gate on each so later phases never rest on an unvalidated first one, and detail sized to a subagent. [Reduced developer agency is an adoption cost, and planning is its remedy](concepts/reduced-developer-agency-is-an-adoption-cost-and-planning-is-its-remedy.md) names the cost no throughput metric reports — engineers who took pleasure in writing code now "wait on output from AI" and burn out — and proposes relocating the craft rather than retreating from the tools. And [mark which lines a human wrote so readers can budget attention](concepts/mark-which-lines-a-human-wrote-so-readers-can-budget-attention.md) answers documents that grew "three or four times as long" while saying the same thing, with a convention rather than a tool, and with a caution: a provenance label may route attention and may never grant acceptance.
- [An AI-Infrastructure Package Is a High-Yield Credential Target](concepts/an-ai-infrastructure-package-is-a-high-yield-credential-target.md) - Saoud Rizwan (Cline) supplies this wiki's first concrete supply-chain incident inside the agent stack, and its lesson is about who got hit. LiteLLM — "3.5 million downloads a day" — was published from a compromised GitHub app, and for roughly three hours its packages carried a credential harvester and an RCE backdoor, found only because the malware crashed Cursor when someone ran the LiteLLM MCP server. The victims were "enterprise customers and developers that have their own internal gateways": self-hosting a gateway to control your keys is the same act as concentrating them, distinguished only by whether the gateway stays trustworthy. Three arguments travel with it, each from a founder with a direct stake. [A subsidized coding-agent subscription is a lock-in ramp](concepts/a-subsidized-coding-agent-subscription-is-a-lock-in-ramp.md): a $200 plan estimated at $8,000–$14,000 of API value against "$500 million on subscriptions" in one month is adoption bought below cost, and the exposure accrues in your harness and habits rather than your invoice — the cheapest audit is a show of hands during a provider outage, and Coinbase halved spend by repointing a gateway default it already had. [Commoditize the layer you do not win on](concepts/commoditize-the-layer-you-do-not-win-on.md) reads open weights through Facebook's Open Compute Project, on the claim that "the industry will adopt and standardize on something that they can build on top of even if it isn't the best thing," with weights published and research held back because "that's what gives us the lead." And [closing the contribution channel is where slop filtering ends](concepts/closing-the-contribution-channel-is-where-slop-filtering-ends.md) traces the one-directional escalation from templates to Zig's ban, curl's bug bounty, and tldraw's closed pull requests — the cost being the apprenticeship path, with no replacement proposed. A keynote three days after launching an open-weights subscription: every third-party figure relayed from a slide, the 2030 projections unattributed, the no-leapfrog bound asserted and contested, and the head-to-head that anchors the harness argument a single bug run once per model.
- [Bound What an Unattended Automation May Emit, Including Emitting Nothing](concepts/bound-what-an-unattended-automation-may-emit.md) - Idan Gazit (GitHub Next) supplies the control this wiki's security and agent material has been missing a name for. Everything else here bounds what an agent may *reach*; a workflow's front-matter manifest also bounds what a run may *produce*, by kind and by count — "Pull request single… because I don't want the agent to get prompt injected to create 500 pull requests. That would be a denial of service" — and grants permission to produce nothing at all, because "the last thing I want is noise. I don't want the agents denial of servicing me." Two more ideas travel with it: [what is newly worth automating](concepts/automate-the-chores-that-needed-judgment-not-rules.md) is the chore no rule could decide but a human decides in seconds (Home Assistant's workflow walking a stack trace to see whose bug it is), and [what a human still has to say](concepts/tell-the-agent-only-what-is-not-recoverable-from-the-code.md) is defined by subtraction — "anything that's in code, any fact that's in code, the agents can figure out by reading the code. What's left are the things that are not in code." All of it is a vendor lab demo with nothing measured.
- [Read the Stop Reason Before You Read the Answer](concepts/read-the-stop-reason-before-you-read-the-answer.md) - Frank Coyle (UC Berkeley) reads Anthropic's Claude Code certification blueprint as an engineering document rather than an exam syllabus, on the argument that a vendor's exam weights encode what its support volume actually shows: agentic architecture at 27% and harness configuration at 20%, both ahead of prompting, with every scenario question carrying a named wrong answer you can check a repository against ([read a certification blueprint as a map of production anti-patterns](concepts/read-a-certification-blueprint-as-a-map-of-production-anti-patterns.md)). The most immediately usable correction is control flow: an agent loop branches on the API's `stop_reason`, not on the text it just received — `tool_use` executes and loops, `end_turn` exits, and `max_tokens` means the response was cut mid-sentence, "which happens more often than you might think." That third case is the one worth auditing today, because a truncated answer carries no marker in its body and is indistinguishable from a short one to any harness that parses content. He also names a failure mode for multi-agent review that this wiki's parallel-and-merge material does not: agents which read each other's reasoning "devolve into one idea," so a critic should get the claim and the evidence and be denied "the thought processes that went in to creating this claim" ([withhold the producer's reasoning from the critic](concepts/withhold-the-producers-reasoning-from-the-critic.md)) — a fourth independence axis alongside separate context, separate prompt or provider, and separate method class, and the cheapest of the four, since only the reviewer's input changes. On context he prescribes two bounds rather than one, forking the subtask so its tool traffic never enters the parent and then compacting the parent on a token threshold, because neither substitutes for the other ([bound context twice: fork the subtask, then compact on a token threshold](concepts/bound-context-twice-fork-the-subtask-then-compact-on-a-token-threshold.md)); "just because you have a million tokens doesn't mean you should use it." Two operational notes travel on their own: interactive mode left on in CI is an anti-pattern because the agent halts for a permission prompt nobody will answer, and any batch of work that does not need an answer today runs at half the token cost with a 24-hour turnaround. Read all of it as a professor's reading of a published vendor blueprint — nothing measured, several prescriptions attributed to Anthropic without citation, and the whole scoped to one harness.
- [Audit a Refactor Against Having Waited for Better Models](concepts/audit-a-refactor-against-having-waited-for-better-models.md) - Denys Linkov (WiseDocs) spent six months collapsing more than ten repositories into a monorepo and built the talk around the question that makes it useful: "should we have waited a year to do this refactor as models and harnesses continue to get better?" He states the case for waiting himself — "taking on technical debt and refactoring later is getting exponentially easier as the days go by" — and answers it not with a capability forecast but with what accrues meanwhile: code "written with low performance or quality" that "people don't actually understand," which is precisely the code you cannot change on a customer's schedule. The decision rule is ROI on debt, and the option the binary framing hides is layered partial isolation. Three measurement practices travel further than the refactor. [Read the task-length curve at the success rate you would actually delegate at](concepts/read-the-task-length-curve-at-the-success-rate-you-would-delegate-at.md): at 80/90/99% "we're no longer claiming that models can accomplish tasks that would take a human 18+ hours," because "if you're kicking off a process that is going to take an hour and it has a 50% chance of completing, there's a very high chance you just wasted that hour." [Re-run one remembered hard task on each new model](concepts/re-run-one-remembered-hard-task-on-each-new-model.md) — o3 in Cursor "took 3 hours of back and forth… but it made 10 major mistakes," Opus 4.8 "was basically able to one-shot this problem" — measured in hours, iterations, and mistakes caught, which dissolves the scorer problem this wiki's PR-replay page leaves open, at the cost of everything statistical and with the harness unpinned. And [validate a research report by building the thing it recommends](concepts/validate-a-research-report-by-building-the-thing-it-recommends.md), against "AI psychosis, where you look at a deep research report that's 20 pages long… And then those features don't actually exist in the product" — the same failure the talk's own zero-shot run produced in code, declaring success in "10 minutes and 22 seconds" over scaffolding with the models missing. The Q&A retires an assumption: [multi-repo cost has moved from navigation to verification](concepts/multi-repo-cost-has-moved-from-navigation-to-verification.md), since agents now read across repositories fine and it is end-to-end testing, deployment, and per-task cloning that still break. An 18-minute practitioner talk, self-reported throughout, that argues AI-era teams ship faster with degrading quality and then measures its own rebuild only in velocity, reach, and cost — with the six months' cost never priced.
- [Separate Execution From the Production Context That Judges It](concepts/separate-execution-from-the-production-context-that-judges-it.md) - Justin Smith (Resolve AI) points the wave at the half of engineering the wiki's coding-agent material keeps deferring: "70% of the time from an engineer is actually not focused just on writing code. It's actually spent on actually running the code that is actually shipped into production," and agents enlarge it, since "AI is creating a lot more issues in production." His analytical cut is that a task is execution plus context, and only the second decides anything — "it's one thing to go check a dashboard. It's another thing to say that metric smells off… you really need that production context that tells you is this important or not important." The workload half turns on a trigger taxonomy: what separates the well-served work from the rest is a ceremony, since "on-call you've got a page that goes off… Incidents you create a bridge," while watching a deploy land healthy or chasing a returning P99 drift has neither, so you [give unowned operational work a trigger](concepts/give-unowned-operational-work-a-trigger.md) — a schedule, an event stream, or a message. Two workloads sharpen existing pages. Verification triggered by the change rather than the symptom [derives the post-deploy check plan from what actually changed](concepts/derive-the-post-deploy-check-plan-from-what-changed.md), reading the diff, following the causal chain, and choosing its own recheck interval ("Maybe I'll come back in 3 days"), which is the only coverage on offer for [the change paths that bypass your deployment pipeline](concepts/watch-the-change-paths-that-bypass-your-deployment-pipeline.md) — feature flags and infra changes that "maybe don't get any monitoring at all." And a channel agent that nobody at-mentioned [answers behind a confidence gate](concepts/answer-unaddressed-questions-behind-a-confidence-gate.md), defaulting to silence and DMing to confirm when unsure, which is a control this wiki has not had: proactivity that can decline. Standing jobs are corrected where their output lands — [amend a recurring agent task by replying to its output](concepts/amend-a-recurring-agent-task-by-replying-to-its-output.md). A ~25-minute vendor talk with no Q&A, an explicitly "fake" demo environment, two segments that never completed on stage, an uncited 70% premise, no measurement of anything, no threshold behind the confidence gate, and no security discussion at all despite agents that read critical Slack channels and trigger on messages anyone can post.
- [Make One Agent Session Reachable From Every Interface](concepts/make-one-agent-session-reachable-from-every-interface.md) - Arjun Singh (Superconductor) reports from a team at "99.9% of our PRs are heavily agent generated" with "everything's human reviewed," and his lessons are mostly about the team. The first is that a chat bot only relocates the constraint — "Claude has a Slack bot. Codex has a Slack bot. We have a Slack bot" takes the agent "from trapped on somebody's laptop to kind of trapped in Slack" — so the object that must be addressable from every surface is the session with its context, which lets a reviewer ask *the session* why a change was made instead of waiting for its author. Underneath every lesson sits one precondition stated as a dependency rather than a benefit: [environment isolation is what lets non-engineers trigger real work](concepts/environment-isolation-is-what-lets-non-engineers-trigger-real-work.md), because "your non-technical people don't have development environments set up on their computers." Its security half is the sharpest correction here to this wiki's sandbox material — [a developer laptop is an ambient-credential surface](concepts/a-developer-laptop-is-an-ambient-credential-surface.md), where the dangerous agent is the obedient one that finds a production token nobody handed it, a case no approval prompt and no per-tool scope can catch. The triage half removes the human selector: MCP solved reading the signals, not choosing among them, so a meeting bot [turns unfiled conversation into concrete prototypes](concepts/turn-unfiled-conversation-into-concrete-prototypes.md) on a contract that the output is reactable rather than shippable. And the model-choice half gives this wiki's leaderboard skepticism a fourth failure mode plus a remedy: [replay your own merged PRs as the coding-agent benchmark](concepts/replay-your-own-merged-prs-as-the-agent-benchmark.md), because "SWE-bench is all in Python, we're Ruby on Rails." A ~19-minute vendor talk whose best artifact, the benchmark plot, is the least reproducible from it — no task count, no rubric, no definition of "quality," and no account of how a replayed attempt is scored.
- [Velocity Sickness Is Output Without Impact](concepts/velocity-sickness-is-output-without-impact.md) - Matt Dailey (Ref) approaches the throughput problem from the direction the entry below does not: not what happens to the quality of what a fast team produces, but what happens to its direction. He names four team-level symptoms of "output without impact" — a PR backlog that breaks the merge queue, work "sprinting in different directions," agent bankruptcy each morning ("you have 12 terminals… it's like walking into just a room of strangers"), and decisions made by agents — three of which no shipping metric can see. The one he ranks first is the one this wiki's controls miss: [ceding a critical decision transfers ownership of the code](concepts/ceding-a-critical-decision-transfers-ownership-of-the-code.md), and reading every line afterwards does not undo it, because the handoff already happened at a "this is the recommended option" prompt — the same in-session gate collapse that OpenAI and Duolingo report from opposite directions. His answer is structural: once agents own implementation, what remains above them is [a decision layer with inverted requirements](concepts/separate-the-decision-layer-from-the-implementation-layer.md), "creative and collaborative" where implementation was "individual" and "heads down," which is why the IDE, chat ("a brain off medium"), plan mode, and spec-driven development are each ruled out by name — and why drift from planning into polish inside one session is the cheap thing to watch for. The mechanism is [making the doc the state and the agent the action](concepts/make-the-doc-the-state-and-the-agent-the-action.md), which puts a stateless-agent argument against this wiki's opposing position that the session transcript is the intent record, and inherits an unpaid bill, since a living state document has no owner, version, or retirement rule. The reported success signal inverts the usual one: teams that install the layer [plan and then do not implement](concepts/unimplemented-plans-signal-a-working-decision-layer.md), escaping "prototype gravity" so prioritization follows exploration. It is a founder's talk for a product in that category, nothing in it is measured, and the one supporting anecdote is second-hand.
- [Verification Debt Outlives the Productivity Spike](concepts/verification-debt-outlives-the-productivity-spike.md) - Anirban Chatterjee (Sonar) gives the wiki's review-capacity and code-quality threads a time axis and a name. A Carnegie Mellon study sorting GitHub projects by whether an AI tool wrote the code reports "a temporary spike in productivity… it lasted about 3 months and then it went back down" alongside "a persistent increase in static analysis warnings and code complexity" that "persisted well into the future" — so the two effects of AI coding decay at different rates, and reading either alone gives the opposite conclusion depending on when you look. The gap that remains is sized by criticality rather than volume, with adversarial users as an axis this wiki's consequence-and-scale framing did not have. Human review is the obvious backstop and he argues it leaks: participants in a Wharton study followed AI advice "92.7% of the time when the AI was correct" and still listened "nearly 80% of the time when the AI was wrong," which he expects to worsen under agentic volume because "the load is just too great. There's only so many hours in the day" — so the failure surfaces as approvals rather than as a queue ([Automation Bias Turns Human-in-the-Loop Into a Rubber Stamp](concepts/automation-bias-turns-human-in-the-loop-into-a-rubber-stamp.md)). What follows are two properties for the automated backstop. [Zero trust](concepts/verify-generated-code-with-a-method-the-generator-does-not-share.md) is provenance-blindness plus method-class independence — "use a different methodology to review the code that was used to write the code" — a stronger claim than the wiki's existing separate-prompt and separate-provider patterns, and its payoff is provability rather than detection rate. [Multi-layered](concepts/choose-verification-layers-by-defect-class-coverage.md) means picking layers by defect class reached, since "software is not provable in the same way that code is." The loop is ACDC, already here from the May 2026 Sonar talk; new is the argument for verifying *inside* the agent's loop, which is [propagation rather than latency](concepts/fix-defects-inside-the-agent-loop-before-they-become-foundation.md). A per-dimension leaderboard also turns the Claude tier toggle into [a quality decision rather than a cost one](concepts/route-between-model-tiers-by-quality-dimension-not-only-cost.md). Read as vendor argument: nothing about Sonar's own loop is measured, neither study is cited with a link or sample size, and the product offered as the independent check can also merge its own fixes automatically.
- [Match Agent Tooling to the Model's Training Distribution](concepts/match-agent-tooling-to-the-models-training-distribution.md) - Dominik Kundel's tour of the Codex harness is the other lab's answer to the stale-harness argument above, and its most transferable claim is that several tooling decisions are set by the weights rather than by taste: apply patch is "a format that we trained the model on starting with GPT-5," ripgrep ships in the binary "because we know the model has been trained on using ripgrep," and auto compaction runs server-side "in a way that the model got trained with." The thinning is real, but the thinned-away logic reappears behind an API on the provider's schedule. The context half answers one problem twice, split on whether a surface must stay visible: tools need not, so definitions are [deferred out of the window and retrieved by a tool-search call](concepts/defer-tool-definitions-out-of-context-and-let-the-model-search-for-them.md); skills must, so the list gets [a hard cap of 2% of the maximum context window](concepts/cap-the-skills-list-as-a-share-of-the-context-window.md) with descriptions trimmed as the catalog grows. The safety half starts from a failure of the human gate — approval prompts produced fatigue, "we saw people would just start clicking yes" — so the first reviewer becomes [a read-only review subagent](concepts/escalate-risky-actions-to-a-read-only-review-subagent.md) judging [how explicitly the user authorized the action](concepts/judge-an-action-by-how-explicitly-the-user-authorized-it.md). Also here: [async work as spawn, send, wait, shut down](concepts/model-async-agent-work-as-spawn-send-wait-shut-down.md), [computer use through a persistent scripting session](concepts/drive-computer-use-through-a-persistent-scripting-session.md), [a loop that ends when the model calls a goal tool](concepts/end-a-long-horizon-loop-with-a-model-called-goal-tool.md), [per-OS sandbox primitives](concepts/sandbox-primitives-are-per-operating-system.md), and the one measured claim — at 1,000 tokens per second on Cerebras, [the bottleneck moves to the network](concepts/fast-inference-moves-the-agent-bottleneck-to-the-network.md). Almost nothing else in the talk is measured, and the speaker opens by calling it "a current state of affairs."
- [Adam Absorption Hides Most Weight Updates From the Served Model](concepts/adam-absorption-hides-most-weight-updates-from-the-served-model.md) - Nan Jiang (Modal) supplies the numerical reason RL rollouts can leave the training cluster: an Adam step is bounded at learning rate times a constant because the optimizer divides by running gradient statistics, which at RL post-training learning rates puts a typical step around three millionths — over 1000x below the rounding boundary of the format the rollout fleet actually serves. Because that boundary scales with weight magnitude while the step does not, small weights move and large weights freeze, and roughly 99% of served weights come out of a step bit-identical. The consequences are the practical half: the sync object becomes a [bitwise-lossless patch](concepts/synchronize-rollout-weights-with-a-bitwise-lossless-patch.md) rather than a 500 GB checkpoint, [lower serving precision shrinks it further](concepts/lower-serving-precision-shrinks-the-weight-sync-patch.md), and a trainer can [publish immutable numbered versions to a bulletin board](concepts/publish-immutable-weight-versions-to-a-bulletin-board.md) that any safetensors-loading engine pulls without RDMA. Read it with the two limits on the page: this is not gradient sparsity (the trainer's update stays dense), and the whole argument is Adam-specific, which Jiang raises himself against Muon adoption at Moonshot and DeepSeek.
- [Own Agent Adoption at the Leadership Layer Because the Fixes Are Shared](concepts/own-agent-adoption-at-the-leadership-layer-because-the-fixes-are-shared.md) - Aditya Khandelwal (Amazon AGI Lab) makes the structural case that coding-agent adoption cannot be pushed down to individuals: the two changes that move it are codebase-wide, and "if you want to change the way your code base is organized, you can't do that as an IC." Left to individuals, the outcome is self-reinforcing in the wrong direction, because the ten-PR engineers generate the diffs the one-PR engineers have to read — "not only can they not ship, they're going to actually see bad code and then curse the agents" — which sharpens the wiki's aggregate review-capacity thread into a distributional one. Two diagnostics come with it: a fear × utilization map whose observed trajectory includes fear *falling* because expectations fell ("I'm not really that scared cuz… it just ships slop"), and a five-symptom checklist whose sharpest entry reads the complaint that today's model "is being really dumb" as a harness signal — "the model didn't change, right? The [harness] may have changed underneath" — the inverse of the stale-harness case below. The mechanics come with numbers this wiki's progressive-disclosure material has lacked: a ~100-line cap on `SKILL.md`, an `AGENTS.md` that is "a thin index," and first-prompt token burn as the test of whether the deferral is real (20–25K unavoidable, 40–50K means "something's wrong"). One team of about ten over a few months, with nothing measured before or after.
- [A harness fix becomes overhead when the model outgrows it](concepts/a-harness-fix-becomes-overhead-when-the-model-outgrows-it.md) - Anthropic's Applied AI team supplies the sentence to keep from this talk — "harnesses encode assumptions about what Claude cannot do on its own… they go stale as models improve" — and then the worked instance with a cost on both sides. Sonnet 4.5 "literally got anxious as it approached its context window limit" and wrapped up tasks early; the team added context-reset machinery; a later model no longer had the behavior and the machinery "became pure overhead, adding things like latency and causing issues with the cache being discarded incorrectly at times." A stale guardrail is not inert scaffolding, because it keeps executing. The architecture half is a decoupling story from a team that shipped the coupled version first: [split the agent loop from the tool execution environment](concepts/decouple-the-agent-loop-from-the-tool-execution-environment.md) because the container was blocking first token and either half dying killed the run — a split the wiki previously had argued only on security grounds. Recovery in both directions then depends on [keeping the session log separate from the context window](concepts/keep-the-session-log-separate-from-the-context-window.md), which also names the assumption behind most compaction pain: where "the context window and the session are one in the same," eviction is deletion. The rest is a hosted-runtime vocabulary ([agent, environment, session](concepts/model-a-managed-agent-as-agent-environment-session.md)), a build-versus-buy checklist ([six production questions](concepts/decide-the-agent-buy-boundary-with-six-production-questions.md)), two security placements ([credentials decrypted only at tool execution](concepts/decrypt-agent-credentials-only-at-tool-execution-time.md), [outbound-only tunnels to private MCP servers](concepts/reach-private-mcp-servers-with-outbound-only-tunnels.md)), and two announced-but-unevaluated features recorded with their gaps: [batch memory rewriting](concepts/rewrite-agent-memory-in-a-periodic-batch-pass-over-session-logs.md) and [an in-loop rubric grader that retries until it passes](concepts/grade-with-a-parallel-rubric-agent-and-retry-until-it-passes.md). A vendor talk whose only numbers are two unmethodized latency percentages — though note a model lab is the one arguing "harnesses have become the limiting factor to what models can achieve."
- [Score a post-training algorithm on four properties](concepts/score-post-training-algorithms-on-four-properties.md) - Ronak Malde (Trajectory) gives the second independent account of hint-based self-distillation at this event, and the transferable parts are a rubric and two failure modes rather than the method. The rubric asks of any post-training algorithm whether the task distribution is online, whether sampling is on-policy, whether parallelism is one, and whether the reward is per token — and its value is that it names what a training choice costs. Parallelism is the row engineers should read first, because group rollouts are what force environments that are "one-to-one copies of the real world," so an environment fleet is a consequence of one algorithmic choice rather than a fixed cost of post-training. The mechanism claim is that [matching a teacher's full next-token distribution shifts distributions rather than sharpening one](concepts/optimize-the-whole-vocabulary-not-the-sampled-token.md), carrying the checkable prediction that tokens-to-solve falls where RL inflates it. More useful than either: the two ways it breaks. [Long horizons collapse into hedging](concepts/long-horizon-self-distillation-collapses-into-hedging.md) — over 50-100 tool calls the output concentrates on "wait," "but," "maybe" — mitigated by [weighting each step's loss by student/teacher KL divergence](concepts/weight-distillation-steps-by-student-teacher-divergence.md) so the signal decays once the rollout leaves the region the teacher's correction applies to. And [hint leakage is the reward hacking of self-distillation](concepts/hint-leakage-is-the-reward-hacking-of-self-distillation.md): a hint containing the resolution teaches the model to state the answer and back-fill the derivation, so hints should carry signals rather than resolutions. The same talk's Q&A deflates its own vision slide — [today's continual learning is batch updates and a model re-upload](concepts/todays-continual-learning-is-batch-updates-and-a-model-reupload.md), with merging 10,000 concurrent rollouts named as unsolved. No measured number appears anywhere in the talk.
- [Raise the floor before maxing the benchmark](concepts/raise-the-floor-before-maxing-the-benchmark.md) - Ben Hylak (Raindrop) splits agent quality into a ceiling, "the craziest capability… that your product or agent is capable of," and a floor, "the worst thing your agent can do," and argues that nearly all published eval effort measures the first while user trust is lost on the second. The floor examples are deliberately ordinary — recommend a competitor, delete a bunch of data, send "an AI slop email to a customer because it like technically had access" — and two of the three follow from access the agent legitimately held rather than from a model error, which makes them product decisions rather than things to wait on a model upgrade for. The two cannot be separated, because the creativity that produces the ceiling is what produces the floor; you can only raise it, and "you want more safety but you don't want theater." The operational half of the same talk says what a floor-raising program needs from its telemetry: [onset and share of users per issue](concepts/triage-agent-issues-by-onset-and-share-of-users.md), which is what [raw clustering cannot give you](concepts/clusters-are-not-issues.md). A vendor talk with no measurements attached to any of it.
- [Measure learning as gain over a memory-wiped rerun](concepts/measure-learning-as-gain-over-a-memory-wiped-rerun.md) - Parth Asawa (UC Berkeley) supplies the instrument this wiki has been missing for every claim that a system "learns from experience": run it through the benchmark twice, once carrying state across instances and once reset between every one, and report the difference. The control removes the confound that "total reward alone might confound continual learning ability with base model strength," and it is deliberately neutral about the mechanism — "it might mean that it's updating its notes. It might mean that it's just growing its context length" — which is exactly what puts notepads, summarizers, retrievers, and a raw growing window on one plot. Three criteria gate the tasks it can be computed on ([headroom that offline training cannot close, structure shared across instances, and feedback in the environment](concepts/a-learning-benchmark-needs-headroom-shared-structure-and-a-signal.md)), and the cheapest shortcut is ruled out outright: [chained independent benchmarks cannot measure learning](concepts/chained-independent-benchmarks-cannot-measure-learning.md), because their instances "are designed to be independent." The result on the resulting benchmark is the uncomfortable part — [plain in-context learning topped the leaderboard](concepts/plain-in-context-learning-topped-a-continual-learning-benchmark.md) on reward, on gain, and on both cost frontiers, beating the memory and context-management systems, on tasks explicitly built to reward accumulation. That is the third independent measurement here, from a third team, where doing less to the context beat doing more; Asawa names the limit himself, calling these "medium horizon tasks" that "didn't push the frontiers of the in-context learning systems enough." Two eval habits travel regardless: [inject concept drift](concepts/inject-concept-drift-to-test-what-a-system-forgets.md) so the suite tests what a system stops believing and not only what it accumulates, and [sort failures as stability or plasticity](concepts/classify-continual-learning-failures-as-stability-or-plasticity.md) — an oscillating forecast versus a notepad agent fluently dismissing a schema that did apply. Read the whole thing knowing that no number is spoken anywhere in the talk and the competing systems are never named.
- [Place a continual-learning setup on two axes](concepts/place-a-continual-learning-setup-on-the-trace-and-hint-axes.md) - Samuel Denton (Applied Compute) replaces "do continual learning" with a 2x2: how on-policy the *trace* is (a one-time production dump → a daily batch → the "unified engine" where serving and training are one loop) against where the *hint* comes from (a static prior about desired behavior vs. one written against the model's own rollout). The axes are independent, and only the first is an infrastructure question — which is why [offline hints on offline traces need no replayable environment](concepts/offline-hints-on-offline-traces-need-no-replayable-environment.md) and why the middle rung, where you [buy on-policyness with a single rollout step](concepts/buy-on-policyness-with-a-single-rollout-step.md), costs one forward pass instead of an environment. The supervision half rests on one move: [distill without a golden answer by giving the teacher privileged information](concepts/distill-without-a-golden-answer-using-privileged-information.md) — make the teacher smarter by what it knows rather than by how large it is, and self-distillation runs with no correct output and no rubric. Two customer results carry the talk. A tool-call rate was raised by conditioning on traces that never contained the call, so the update [moved the reasoning path, not the target tokens](concepts/move-the-reasoning-path-not-the-target-tokens.md) (task-completion ~22%→60%, test pass rate flat) — the weights-layer twin of the wiki's prompt-layer finding that leading words steer behavior through the trace. And on an out-of-distribution formatting requirement, a correct non-hackable reward *and* SFT on correct traces both degraded general coding ability, while [hinting against the model's own prior rollout](concepts/hint-against-the-rollout-when-rewards-and-sft-degrade-the-base-model.md) took correctness ~15%→80%; the same advice applied uniformly barely moved it, which is the case for [letting a judge place the hint and distilling only the steps near it](concepts/let-a-judge-place-the-hint-and-distill-only-nearby-steps.md) and, one scale further in, for [masking irrelevant teacher tokens before learning from them](concepts/mask-irrelevant-teacher-tokens-before-learning-from-them.md). The portable discipline, whether or not you train anything: [measure a behavior change with three metrics including their intersection](concepts/measure-a-behavior-change-with-three-metrics-including-their-intersection.md). Read it as a vendor talk closing on a hiring slide — every number is from unpublished internal customer runs with no seeds, variance, or sample counts, the claim that one on-policy step beats fully-offline is delivered against a chart with no numbers spoken, the failed baselines carry no magnitudes, and nothing prices the online loop against the offline one it is said to beat.
- [A budget stopping rule can masquerade as a capability ceiling](concepts/a-budget-stopping-rule-can-masquerade-as-a-capability-ceiling.md) - Sara Hooker (Adaption, formerly head of Cohere Labs) reports that her company's automated training search beats internal research staff on "60-something, 65%, 63%" of runs, and then supplies the detail that makes the number readable: the search is given a compute budget and stops the moment it beats the baseline. One-sided clustering just above a round number, with nothing below and nothing far above, is the signature of an exit condition, not of a limit — which generalizes past this talk to best-of-N sampling, agent retry loops, and any swept benchmark, and turns into a publishing rule (state the termination criterion, whether it actually bound, and one run with the cap removed). Two stated mechanisms sit behind the search itself: [co-optimize data with the model or the search does not pay](concepts/co-optimize-data-with-the-model-or-the-search-does-not-pay.md), since "we did not get the returns… until you control for data quality," and [the edge over staff is the absence of architecture priors](concepts/automated-training-search-beats-staff-by-not-carrying-architecture-priors.md) — no favorite family to defend, and a willingness to move many hyperparameters at once where a person moves one. The macro claim wrapped around them is that [pre-training size is no longer the most lucrative scaling axis](concepts/pretraining-size-is-no-longer-the-most-lucrative-scaling-axis.md), with post-training "moving further back" — an argument whose evidence is thin (a single Open LLM leaderboard flip at one parameter cut, offered for a claim about whole model classes) and whose consequence is the interesting part: the compute that now carries returns is the [distributable](concepts/distributable-compute-lowers-the-barrier-to-frontier-work.md) kind rather than the colocated kind pre-training needs, which she reads as market entry opening for anyone who can reach the [apprenticed know-how](concepts/frontier-training-know-how-is-apprenticeship-not-literature.md) held by fewer than 5,000 people. Two boundaries drawn in Q&A are worth carrying regardless of what you make of the thesis: [automating harness design requires co-optimizing the model](concepts/automating-harness-design-requires-co-optimizing-the-model.md), because scaffold search against frozen weights converges — a limit on the *automated* loop, not on the hand-tuned harness work this wiki documents throughout — and [customization control is a separate question from open weights](concepts/customization-control-is-a-separate-question-from-open-weights.md), three axes (weight distribution, adaptation control, deployment locality) that policy and procurement language routinely collapse into one. Read the whole thing as a founder talk with a free-GPU beta attached: the win rates and the "outperforms research staff" claim are unpublished, the coverage claims (non-verifiable tasks, 242 languages) are stated intent, and the title's "gradient-free" never appears in the transcript.
- Against a private corpus the frontier playbook does not apply: two of the three scaling axes are already spent, the purchased annotation layer is [public by construction](concepts/post-training-data-is-public-by-construction.md), and every technique for turning your data into model capability — naive training, KV compaction, on-policy distillation, continued pre-training — shares [one ceiling](concepts/the-synthetic-data-wall-caps-every-define-then-train-loop.md) that a first adaptation pass should be budgeted against.
- [Ablate the recall policy with a ladder and an oracle](concepts/ablate-the-recall-policy-with-a-ladder-and-an-oracle.md) - Stefania Druga (Sakana AI) ran the memory experiment this wiki had been describing but never measuring: hold the model, task, core context, and archival store fixed, vary only the recall block, and climb a ladder from no recall at all, through vector similarity, through a ranked ledger of per-turn decisions, up to an oracle handed the correct memory for every loop. Her framing is that [memory is a write–manage–read control loop, not a store](concepts/treat-memory-as-a-write-manage-read-control-loop.md) — which is exactly what makes each rung an ablatable policy. Four results are worth carrying. [A memory harness adds only cost when the task fits in context](concepts/a-memory-harness-adds-only-cost-when-the-task-fits-in-context.md): on a literature review whose corpus fit in the window — with a deliberately hard needle, a retracted Nature claim buried under its own headlines — it was "the same performance with memory and without memory, and it only added more cost," while on an xbench question whose answer sat at step 124 and whose question arrived at step 500 the harness was the only route to the answer. [A ranked decisions ledger beat retrieving by similarity](concepts/rank-a-decisions-ledger-instead-of-retrieving-memories-by-similarity.md), and survived being fed the wrong step, the most recent step, and arbitrary examples — an independent convergence on the wiki's existing objection to similarity-only memory, reached by changing the *unit* stored rather than by learning the ranking from outcomes. [Do not gate memory on the agent's own judgment](concepts/do-not-gate-memory-use-on-the-agents-own-judgment.md): asking the model whether it needed memory lost to always-on ranked recall, which is the same weakness the oracle exposes from the other side — [giving a model the right memory does not make it use the right memory](concepts/an-oracle-ceiling-separates-retrieval-failure-from-use-failure.md), so the gap above the oracle is not recoverable by any retriever. And the line worth keeping: [bad recall costs more than no recall](concepts/bad-recall-costs-more-than-no-recall.md), because the accurate policy was also the cheapest — wrong context is charged in tokens *and* in the turns spent down the path it suggested. Read it as a template rather than a result: single-author, unpublished, on local quantized models, [which is itself the point she makes about owning every step of the pipeline](concepts/run-harness-ablations-on-local-models-to-own-every-step.md), and reporting orderings with no effect sizes anywhere.
- [Separate intelligence from expertise when diagnosing an agent](concepts/separate-intelligence-from-expertise-when-diagnosing-agents.md) - Yu Su (NeoCognition) argues the two are roughly orthogonal: intelligence is reasoning through an unfamiliar problem from the context you were handed, where "every episode is more or less independent," while expertise is accumulated, situated competence in one environment. Scale only the first and you get "the world's smartest novice," brute-forcing every problem and keeping nothing between them. His explanation for why coding agents escaped this is [a modern Moravec's paradox](concepts/a-modern-moravecs-paradox-explains-the-coding-agent-gap.md) — code is "already a language-native world," symbolic, structured, and shipped with tests standing in for rewards, which reads as a transfer checklist for any new agent domain — while everything else is [millions of microworlds with their own local physics](concepts/digital-work-is-millions-of-microworlds-with-local-physics.md), where even identical software is configured differently per company and the variety is "too heterogeneous and dynamic for any monolithic model to try to compress… into one static representation." Two pieces are immediately usable regardless of what you make of the thesis. [Expertise compresses the search while intelligence expands it](concepts/expertise-compresses-the-search-intelligence-expands-it.md) — a hundred parallel attempts is the price of having no learned shortcuts — and expertise is also what selects which context to bring, which puts context engineering in the position of externalizing a competence the model lacks. And [continual learning gets a definition worth borrowing](concepts/define-continual-learning-as-adaptive-compression-of-experience.md): "adaptive compression of experience into reusable structures for future behavior," four axes that make the wiki's running profiles, replayable failure environments, skills, and distillation comparable for the first time — they share the label and almost no machinery. The unresolved parts are stated as such: [reliability and plasticity conflict](concepts/reliability-and-plasticity-conflict-in-continually-learning-agents.md) by construction, measuring expertise is "probably environment-specific" and unsolved, and the closing provocation — [scale expertise once intelligence is abundant](concepts/scale-expertise-once-intelligence-is-abundant.md), because past some capability threshold "we don't need a stronger intelligence anymore" — is a conjecture with no threshold named. Nothing in the talk is measured, and the speaker is COO of a continual-learning company whose positioning is the conclusion.
- [Optimize capture bandwidth before note organization](concepts/optimize-capture-bandwidth-before-note-organization.md) - Ben Holmes (Warp) walks a working personal knowledge base end to end and puts the unglamorous half first: the binding constraint is how much raw material you get down, not how well it is filed, so dictate at roughly 200 words per minute and let the notes be sloppy, because "you need a lot of raw materials for this to work" and the agent cleans up afterward. It is an independent arrival at the same conclusion as the wiki's existing [drive agents remotely and by voice](concepts/drive-agents-remotely-and-by-voice-to-decouple-work-from-the-desk.md) material, reached from note-taking rather than from mobility. The enrichment pass is a non-coding [agent skill](concepts/agent-skills-package-progressive-disclosure-context-for-repeatable-workflows.md) that summarizes, tags, and links, and its two most portable details are policy-shaped rather than model-shaped: [constrain the tags to a reference vocabulary](concepts/constrain-agent-generated-tags-to-a-reference-vocabulary.md) shipped alongside the skill, because "be reluctant to add new tags — Claude loves to get creative" and an unconstrained tagger dissolves its own index; and [stamp the processing state into the artifact itself](concepts/stamp-processing-state-in-the-artifact-to-make-agent-passes-resumable.md), so an enrichment timestamp in the note's frontmatter is the entire checkpoint store and each pass touches only what changed — with the caveat this wiki should record, that a timestamp cannot invalidate old notes when the skill itself changes, which a version stamp could. Retrieval is deliberately the boring option: [materialize backlinks at ingest by key-term search](concepts/materialize-backlinks-at-ingest-with-key-term-search.md) rather than standing up embeddings, presented as a complexity tradeoff and not as a demonstrated equivalence. On top he generates two derived layers — [an entity wiki over your own notes](concepts/generate-an-entity-wiki-over-your-own-notes.md) covering people, concepts, organizations, and sources, the third independent build in this wiki from Karpathy's LLM-wiki gist, and [disposable visualizations that expose gaps in the corpus](concepts/generate-disposable-visualizations-to-find-gaps-in-your-own-corpus.md) ("this is not a tool that you have to install… I told an agent, build this for me"). The operational lesson is scheduling: local automations mean "your laptop has to be cracked open," so [run the recurring pass in a cloud sandbox that syncs down and syncs back](concepts/run-recurring-knowledge-jobs-in-a-cloud-sandbox-with-sync-down-sync-back.md). Read it as a shipped demonstration, not as evidence: this is a Warp DevRel talk whose harness, scheduler, and notes app are all first-party, and nothing in it is measured — no error rates, no costs, no comparison against the alternatives it declines.
